People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Um… yeah, thanks. I guess what brings me in is I’ve been having trouble keeping my thoughts, or my words, kind of in order. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think they feel connected when they’re in my head… at least at first. Like, I know why an overnight shift makes me think of patient charts, and then maybe the chart color makes me think of paint colors, and then somehow soccer, because of a jersey or something. It makes sense to me for a second.

But when I say it out loud, it comes out… not in the right order, I guess. Like I can’t keep my words in a straight line. I’ll start one place and end up somewhere totally different, and then I notice people looking confused. I’m not trying to be difficult or weird or anything. I just… I lose the line while I’m talking. Is that the kind of thing you mean?

Yeah… I think that’s kind of what I was trying to say before.

Do you feel like your thoughts are connected or do they feel jumbled or disconnected?\n\nPatient: I think they feel connected when they’re in my head… at least at first. Like, I know why an overnight shift makes me think of patient charts, and then maybe the chart color makes me think of paint colors, and then somehow soccer, because of a jersey or something. It makes sense to me for a second.\n\nBut when I say it out loud, it comes out… not in the right order, I guess. Like I can’t keep my words in a straight line. I’ll start one place and end up somewhere totally different, and then I notice people looking confused. I’m not trying to be difficult or weird or anything. I just… I lose the line while I’m talking. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your head by someone else, or that your thoughts are being taken out or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: Um… not exactly like thoughts being put in my head, I don’t think. Or broadcast, no… I mean, I hope not.\n\nIt’s more like… I’m pretty sure my supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the unit cameras, like to see if I’m unsafe or something. People say it’s just staffing shortages, and I try to accept that, but it doesn’t really feel like that to me. It feels like they’re checking on me.  \n\nI know that might sound… paranoid or bad. I’m not trying to cause trouble. That’s just how it seems.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, or like they're being removed or spread to other people?\n\nPatient: Not really like… inserted or removed, no. I don’t think it’s that exactly.\n\nIt’s more that I’m sure my supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the unit cameras, to see if I’m unsafe or messing up. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, and I get that maybe that’s what they want me to think… sorry, I know that probably sounds bad. But it really feels like they’re checking on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like something on TV or something someone says, are directed at you personally or have a special meaning for you that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Kind of… not so much TV, I don’t think. It’s more at work.\n\nLike when my shifts get changed, or when I notice the unit cameras, it feels like it’s about me. Like my supervisors are trying to check if I’m unsafe or if I’m doing something wrong. People tell me it’s just staffing stuff, and I try to hear that, I really do… but it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels like there’s a reason, and that reason is me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions, such as what you say or do, are being controlled by an outside force or that someone else is making you speak or act in a certain way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think someone is making me talk or move my body, no… not like that.\n\nIt’s more like at work, I feel like what I do is being watched really closely. Like my supervisors changed my shifts and keep an eye on me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. Even when people say it’s just staffing shortages, I try to accept that, but… it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nSorry, I know that may not exactly answer what you mean. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Outside of feeling watched or that changes at work are about you, have you ever felt that your thoughts are being controlled, influenced, or placed into your mind by something or someone else?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I have thoughts that get stuck in my head sometimes, but they feel like mine.\n\nI haven’t felt like somebody is putting them there or controlling them. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, gestures, or comments happening around you were specifically directed at you or had a hidden meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s kind of what I was trying to say before.\n\nI don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m blaming people, but at work it feels… directed at me. Like when my supervisors change my shifts, I’m pretty sure it isn’t just random staffing stuff. And the unit cameras—I feel like they’re watching me through them to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nPeople tell me it’s probably just normal scheduling or shortages, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels like there’s a hidden reason, and that reason is me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something happening on their own, were actually connected to you or happening because of you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so, but mostly it’s the work stuff I keep coming back to.\n\nLike, the shift changes don’t feel random to me. I feel pretty sure my supervisors changed them because of me, and that they’re watching me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nI know people tell me it’s just staffing shortages, and I try to hear that… I don’t want to be difficult. But it still feels connected to me, like there’s a reason behind it. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m trying to make sense, I just feel like my words don’t stay in a straight line lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what goes through your mind when you start talking about one thing and then end up somewhere else? Do you feel like your thoughts are connected or do they feel jumbled or disconnected?\n\nPatient: I think they feel connected when they’re in my head… at least at first. Like, I know why an overnight shift makes me think of patient charts, and then maybe the chart color makes me think of paint colors, and then somehow soccer, because of a jersey or something. It makes sense to me for a second.\n\nBut when I say it out loud, it comes out… not in the right order, I guess. Like I can’t keep my words in a straight line. I’ll start one place and end up somewhere totally different, and then I notice people looking confused. I’m not trying to be difficult or weird or anything. I just… I lose the line while I’m talking. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your head by someone else, or that your thoughts are being taken out or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: Um… not exactly like thoughts being put in my head, I don’t think. Or broadcast, no… I mean, I hope not.\n\nIt’s more like… I’m pretty sure my supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the unit cameras, like to see if I’m unsafe or something. People say it’s just staffing shortages, and I try to accept that, but it doesn’t really feel like that to me. It feels like they’re checking on me.  \n\nI know that might sound… paranoid or bad. I’m not trying to cause trouble. That’s just how it seems.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, or like they're being removed or spread to other people?\n\nPatient: Not really like… inserted or removed, no. I don’t think it’s that exactly.\n\nIt’s more that I’m sure my supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the unit cameras, to see if I’m unsafe or messing up. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, and I get that maybe that’s what they want me to think… sorry, I know that probably sounds bad. But it really feels like they’re checking on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like something on TV or something someone says, are directed at you personally or have a special meaning for you that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Kind of… not so much TV, I don’t think. It’s more at work.\n\nLike when my shifts get changed, or when I notice the unit cameras, it feels like it’s about me. Like my supervisors are trying to check if I’m unsafe or if I’m doing something wrong. People tell me it’s just staffing stuff, and I try to hear that, I really do… but it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels like there’s a reason, and that reason is me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions, such as what you say or do, are being controlled by an outside force or that someone else is making you speak or act in a certain way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think someone is making me talk or move my body, no… not like that.\n\nIt’s more like at work, I feel like what I do is being watched really closely. Like my supervisors changed my shifts and keep an eye on me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. Even when people say it’s just staffing shortages, I try to accept that, but… it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nSorry, I know that may not exactly answer what you mean. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Outside of feeling watched or that changes at work are about you, have you ever felt that your thoughts are being controlled, influenced, or placed into your mind by something or someone else?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I have thoughts that get stuck in my head sometimes, but they feel like mine.\n\nI haven’t felt like somebody is putting them there or controlling them. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, gestures, or comments happening around you were specifically directed at you or had a hidden meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s kind of what I was trying to say before.\n\nI don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m blaming people, but at work it feels… directed at me. Like when my supervisors change my shifts, I’m pretty sure it isn’t just random staffing stuff. And the unit cameras—I feel like they’re watching me through them to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nPeople tell me it’s probably just normal scheduling or shortages, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels like there’s a hidden reason, and that reason is me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something happening on their own, were actually connected to you or happening because of you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so, but mostly it’s the work stuff I keep coming back to.\n\nLike, the shift changes don’t feel random to me. I feel pretty sure my supervisors changed them because of me, and that they’re watching me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nI know people tell me it’s just staffing shortages, and I try to hear that… I don’t want to be difficult. But it still feels connected to me, like there’s a reason behind it. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do or say around you are secretly about you or meant to send you a message that others don’t notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but at work especially, it feels like what they’re doing is about me.\n\nLike the shift changes… I’m pretty sure my supervisors changed them because of me, not just because of staffing. And the cameras on the unit—I feel like they’re watching me through them, like checking if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nPeople keep telling me it’s probably just shortages or normal stuff, and I try to go along with that, but it still feels like there’s a message in it, like it’s aimed at me. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t want to say they’re trying to harm me exactly… I know that sounds serious, and I don’t want to accuse anyone unfairly.

But I do feel pretty sure they changed my shifts because of me. And with the unit cameras, it feels like they’re watching me through them to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. People keep telling me it’s just staffing shortages or normal monitoring, and I try to accept that, but it still feels like there’s a plan around me somehow.

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not like… a device or energy controlling my thoughts, no. I don’t want to make it sound like that.

It’s more the thing I already mentioned—at work, I feel sure my supervisors changed my shifts because of me, and that they’re watching me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. I know people say it’s just staffing shortages or normal cameras, and I’m trying to be reasonable about it… but it still feels true to me.

I’m not trying to cause trouble. That’s just how it seems.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, or like they're being removed or spread to other people?\n\nPatient: Not really like… inserted or removed, no. But it really feels like they’re checking on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like something on TV or something someone says, are directed at you personally or have a special meaning for you that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Kind of… not so much TV, I don’t think. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, thanks. I guess what brings me in is I’ve been having trouble keeping my thoughts, or my words, kind of in order. It’s probably not as bad as it sounds, but I’ll start talking about something normal, like an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or paint colors, or patient charts, and I can tell people are… not really following me anymore.  \n\nI don’t mean to do it. I’m trying to make sense, I just feel like my words don’t stay in a straight line lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what goes through your mind when you start talking about one thing and then end up somewhere else? Do you feel like your thoughts are connected or do they feel jumbled or disconnected?\n\nPatient: I think they feel connected when they’re in my head… at least at first. Like, I know why an overnight shift makes me think of patient charts, and then maybe the chart color makes me think of paint colors, and then somehow soccer, because of a jersey or something. It makes sense to me for a second.\n\nBut when I say it out loud, it comes out… not in the right order, I guess. Like I can’t keep my words in a straight line. I’ll start one place and end up somewhere totally different, and then I notice people looking confused. I’m not trying to be difficult or weird or anything. I just… I lose the line while I’m talking. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your head by someone else, or that your thoughts are being taken out or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: Um… not exactly like thoughts being put in my head, I don’t think. Or broadcast, no… I mean, I hope not.\n\nIt’s more like… I’m pretty sure my supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the unit cameras, like to see if I’m unsafe or something. People say it’s just staffing shortages, and I try to accept that, but it doesn’t really feel like that to me. It feels like they’re checking on me.  \n\nI know that might sound… paranoid or bad. I’m not trying to cause trouble. That’s just how it seems.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, or like they're being removed or spread to other people?\n\nPatient: Not really like… inserted or removed, no. I don’t think it’s that exactly.\n\nIt’s more that I’m sure my supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the unit cameras, to see if I’m unsafe or messing up. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, and I get that maybe that’s what they want me to think… sorry, I know that probably sounds bad. But it really feels like they’re checking on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like something on TV or something someone says, are directed at you personally or have a special meaning for you that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Kind of… not so much TV, I don’t think. It’s more at work.\n\nLike when my shifts get changed, or when I notice the unit cameras, it feels like it’s about me. Like my supervisors are trying to check if I’m unsafe or if I’m doing something wrong. People tell me it’s just staffing stuff, and I try to hear that, I really do… but it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels like there’s a reason, and that reason is me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions, such as what you say or do, are being controlled by an outside force or that someone else is making you speak or act in a certain way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think someone is making me talk or move my body, no… not like that.\n\nIt’s more like at work, I feel like what I do is being watched really closely. Like my supervisors changed my shifts and keep an eye on me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. Even when people say it’s just staffing shortages, I try to accept that, but… it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nSorry, I know that may not exactly answer what you mean. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Outside of feeling watched or that changes at work are about you, have you ever felt that your thoughts are being controlled, influenced, or placed into your mind by something or someone else?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I have thoughts that get stuck in my head sometimes, but they feel like mine.\n\nI haven’t felt like somebody is putting them there or controlling them. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, gestures, or comments happening around you were specifically directed at you or had a hidden meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s kind of what I was trying to say before.\n\nI don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m blaming people, but at work it feels… directed at me. Like when my supervisors change my shifts, I’m pretty sure it isn’t just random staffing stuff. And the unit cameras—I feel like they’re watching me through them to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nPeople tell me it’s probably just normal scheduling or shortages, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels like there’s a hidden reason, and that reason is me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something happening on their own, were actually connected to you or happening because of you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so, but mostly it’s the work stuff I keep coming back to.\n\nLike, the shift changes don’t feel random to me. I feel pretty sure my supervisors changed them because of me, and that they’re watching me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nI know people tell me it’s just staffing shortages, and I try to hear that… I don’t want to be difficult. But it still feels connected to me, like there’s a reason behind it. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do or say around you are secretly about you or meant to send you a message that others don’t notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but at work especially, it feels like what they’re doing is about me.\n\nLike the shift changes… I’m pretty sure my supervisors changed them because of me, not just because of staffing. And the cameras on the unit—I feel like they’re watching me through them, like checking if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nPeople keep telling me it’s probably just shortages or normal stuff, and I try to go along with that, but it still feels like there’s a message in it, like it’s aimed at me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your supervisors are deliberately trying to harm you, or that they have a plan to monitor or control you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to say they’re trying to harm me exactly… I know that sounds serious, and I don’t want to accuse anyone unfairly.\n\nBut I do feel pretty sure they changed my shifts because of me. And with the unit cameras, it feels like they’re watching me through them to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. People keep telling me it’s just staffing shortages or normal monitoring, and I try to accept that, but it still feels like there’s a plan around me somehow.\n\nIs that… is that what you mean by monitoring?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that an external force or power, like a device or energy, is controlling your thoughts or actions, even if not related to your supervisors?\n\nPatient: Not like… a device or energy controlling my thoughts, no. I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nIt’s more the thing I already mentioned—at work, I feel sure my supervisors changed my shifts because of me, and that they’re watching me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. I know people say it’s just staffing shortages or normal cameras, and I’m trying to be reasonable about it… but it still feels true to me.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean, or is that different?\n\nDoctor: Outside of work situations, have you ever felt that an outside force or power is influencing your thoughts or actions in any way?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… I don’t think so. Not really.\n\nI mean, I get anxious about stuff, but I haven’t noticed anything like a force or power making me think things or do things. I don’t want to say it wrong, but no, that doesn’t sound like what happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being influenced or controlled by something inside your body, like a physical force or energy, even if it’s not connected to anyone watching you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve felt anything like that inside my body.\n\nSometimes I get tense or worked up, but not like an energy or force controlling my thoughts. I haven’t noticed that. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, gestures, or comments happening around you were specifically directed at you or had a hidden meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s kind of what I was trying to say before.\n\nI don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m blaming people, but at work it feels… directed at me. Like when my supervisors change my shifts, I’m pretty sure it isn’t just random staffing stuff. And the unit cameras—I feel like they’re watching me through them to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nPeople tell me it’s probably just normal scheduling or shortages, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels like there’s a hidden reason, and that reason is me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something happening on their own, were actually connected to you or happening because of you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so, but mostly it’s the work stuff I keep coming back to.\n\nLike, the shift changes don’t feel random to me. I feel pretty sure my supervisors changed them because of me, and that they’re watching me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nI know people tell me it’s just staffing shortages, and I try to hear that… I don’t want to be difficult. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really with TV or the news, no… I don’t want to say everything is about me or anything like that.

It’s more the stuff at work that feels personal. Like when my supervisors change my shifts, I’m sure it’s not just random staffing, even though people keep telling me that. And the unit cameras… I feel like they’re watching me to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.

I know that might sound like I’m reading into it, but it feels pretty real to me. Is that answering what you mean?

I’m not trying to cause trouble. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels like there’s a reason, and that reason is me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions, such as what you say or do, are being controlled by an outside force or that someone else is making you speak or act in a certain way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think someone is making me talk or move my body, no… not like that.\n\nIt’s more like at work, I feel like what I do is being watched really closely. Like my supervisors changed my shifts and keep an eye on me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Outside of feeling watched or that changes at work are about you, have you ever felt that your thoughts are being controlled, influenced, or placed into your mind by something or someone else?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, gestures, or comments happening around you were specifically directed at you or had a hidden meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s kind of what I was trying to say before.\n\nI don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m blaming people, but at work it feels… directed at me. Like when my supervisors change my shifts, I’m pretty sure it isn’t just random staffing stuff. And the unit cameras—I feel like they’re watching me through them to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nPeople tell me it’s probably just normal scheduling or shortages, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels like there’s a hidden reason, and that reason is me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something happening on their own, were actually connected to you or happening because of you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so, but mostly it’s the work stuff I keep coming back to.\n\nLike, the shift changes don’t feel random to me. I feel pretty sure my supervisors changed them because of me, and that they’re watching me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nI know people tell me it’s just staffing shortages, and I try to hear that… I don’t want to be difficult. But it still feels connected to me, like there’s a reason behind it. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do or say around you are secretly about you or meant to send you a message that others don’t notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. That’s just how it seems.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, or like they're being removed or spread to other people?\n\nPatient: Not really like… inserted or removed, no. I don’t think it’s that exactly.\n\nIt’s more that I’m sure my supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the unit cameras, to see if I’m unsafe or messing up. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, and I get that maybe that’s what they want me to think… sorry, I know that probably sounds bad. But it really feels like they’re checking on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like something on TV or something someone says, are directed at you personally or have a special meaning for you that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Kind of… not so much TV, I don’t think. It’s more at work.\n\nLike when my shifts get changed, or when I notice the unit cameras, it feels like it’s about me. Like my supervisors are trying to check if I’m unsafe or if I’m doing something wrong. People tell me it’s just staffing stuff, and I try to hear that, I really do… but it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels like there’s a reason, and that reason is me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions, such as what you say or do, are being controlled by an outside force or that someone else is making you speak or act in a certain way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think someone is making me talk or move my body, no… not like that.\n\nIt’s more like at work, I feel like what I do is being watched really closely. Like my supervisors changed my shifts and keep an eye on me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. Even when people say it’s just staffing shortages, I try to accept that, but… it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nSorry, I know that may not exactly answer what you mean. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Outside of feeling watched or that changes at work are about you, have you ever felt that your thoughts are being controlled, influenced, or placed into your mind by something or someone else?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I have thoughts that get stuck in my head sometimes, but they feel like mine.\n\nI haven’t felt like somebody is putting them there or controlling them. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, gestures, or comments happening around you were specifically directed at you or had a hidden meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s kind of what I was trying to say before.\n\nI don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m blaming people, but at work it feels… directed at me. Like when my supervisors change my shifts, I’m pretty sure it isn’t just random staffing stuff. And the unit cameras—I feel like they’re watching me through them to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nPeople tell me it’s probably just normal scheduling or shortages, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels like there’s a hidden reason, and that reason is me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something happening on their own, were actually connected to you or happening because of you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so, but mostly it’s the work stuff I keep coming back to.\n\nLike, the shift changes don’t feel random to me. I feel pretty sure my supervisors changed them because of me, and that they’re watching me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nI know people tell me it’s just staffing shortages, and I try to hear that… I don’t want to be difficult. But it still feels connected to me, like there’s a reason behind it. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do or say around you are secretly about you or meant to send you a message that others don’t notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but at work especially, it feels like what they’re doing is about me.\n\nLike the shift changes… I’m pretty sure my supervisors changed them because of me, not just because of staffing. And the cameras on the unit—I feel like they’re watching me through them, like checking if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nPeople keep telling me it’s probably just shortages or normal stuff, and I try to go along with that, but it still feels like there’s a message in it, like it’s aimed at me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your supervisors are deliberately trying to harm you, or that they have a plan to monitor or control you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to say they’re trying to harm me exactly… I know that sounds serious, and I don’t want to accuse anyone unfairly.\n\nBut I do feel pretty sure they changed my shifts because of me. And with the unit cameras, it feels like they’re watching me through them to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. People keep telling me it’s just staffing shortages or normal monitoring, and I try to accept that, but it still feels like there’s a plan around me somehow.\n\nIs that… is that what you mean by monitoring?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that an external force or power, like a device or energy, is controlling your thoughts or actions, even if not related to your supervisors?\n\nPatient: Not like… a device or energy controlling my thoughts, no. I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nIt’s more the thing I already mentioned—at work, I feel sure my supervisors changed my shifts because of me, and that they’re watching me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. I know people say it’s just staffing shortages or normal cameras, and I’m trying to be reasonable about it… but it still feels true to me.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean, or is that different?\n\nDoctor: Outside of work situations, have you ever felt that an outside force or power is influencing your thoughts or actions in any way?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… I don’t think so. Not really.\n\nI mean, I get anxious about stuff, but I haven’t noticed anything like a force or power making me think things or do things. I don’t want to say it wrong, but no, that doesn’t sound like what happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being influenced or controlled by something inside your body, like a physical force or energy, even if it’s not connected to anyone watching you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve felt anything like that inside my body.\n\nSometimes I get tense or worked up, but not like an energy or force controlling my thoughts. I haven’t noticed that. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: When your thoughts jump from one topic to another, like from an overnight shift to paint colors or soccer, does it feel like there’s a reason behind the connection, or does it sometimes feel random even to you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes there is a reason, at least in my head when I’m saying it. It just feels like there’s a reason, and that reason is me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions, such as what you say or do, are being controlled by an outside force or that someone else is making you speak or act in a certain way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think someone is making me talk or move my body, no… not like that.\n\nIt’s more like at work, I feel like what I do is being watched really closely. Like my supervisors changed my shifts and keep an eye on me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. Even when people say it’s just staffing shortages, I try to accept that, but… it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nSorry, I know that may not exactly answer what you mean. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Outside of feeling watched or that changes at work are about you, have you ever felt that your thoughts are being controlled, influenced, or placed into your mind by something or someone else?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I have thoughts that get stuck in my head sometimes, but they feel like mine.\n\nI haven’t felt like somebody is putting them there or controlling them. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, gestures, or comments happening around you were specifically directed at you or had a hidden meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s kind of what I was trying to say before.\n\nI don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m blaming people, but at work it feels… directed at me. Like when my supervisors change my shifts, I’m pretty sure it isn’t just random staffing stuff. And the unit cameras—I feel like they’re watching me through them to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nPeople tell me it’s probably just normal scheduling or shortages, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels like there’s a hidden reason, and that reason is me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something happening on their own, were actually connected to you or happening because of you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so, but mostly it’s the work stuff I keep coming back to.\n\nLike, the shift changes don’t feel random to me. I feel pretty sure my supervisors changed them because of me, and that they’re watching me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nI know people tell me it’s just staffing shortages, and I try to hear that… I don’t want to be difficult. But it still feels connected to me, like there’s a reason behind it. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do or say around you are secretly about you or meant to send you a message that others don’t notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but at work especially, it feels like what they’re doing is about me.\n\nLike the shift changes… I’m pretty sure my supervisors changed them because of me, not just because of staffing. And the cameras on the unit—I feel like they’re watching me through them, like checking if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong.\n\nPeople keep telling me it’s probably just shortages or normal stuff, and I try to go along with that, but it still feels like there’s a message in it, like it’s aimed at me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your supervisors are deliberately trying to harm you, or that they have a plan to monitor or control you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to say they’re trying to harm me exactly… I know that sounds serious, and I don’t want to accuse anyone unfairly.\n\nBut I do feel pretty sure they changed my shifts because of me. And with the unit cameras, it feels like they’re watching me through them to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. People keep telling me it’s just staffing shortages or normal monitoring, and I try to accept that, but it still feels like there’s a plan around me somehow.\n\nIs that… is that what you mean by monitoring?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that an external force or power, like a device or energy, is controlling your thoughts or actions, even if not related to your supervisors?\n\nPatient: Not like… a device or energy controlling my thoughts, no. I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nIt’s more the thing I already mentioned—at work, I feel sure my supervisors changed my shifts because of me, and that they’re watching me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. I know people say it’s just staffing shortages or normal cameras, and I’m trying to be reasonable about it… but it still feels true to me.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean, or is that different?\n\nDoctor: Outside of work situations, have you ever felt that an outside force or power is influencing your thoughts or actions in any way?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed that. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: When your thoughts jump from one topic to another, like from an overnight shift to paint colors or soccer, does it feel like there’s a reason behind the connection, or does it sometimes feel random even to you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes there is a reason, at least in my head when I’m saying it.
